Covariant quantization of the Einstein-Hilbert theory in first-order form
S. Martins-Filho

Abstract
We present a covariant quantization of the first-order formulation of the Einstein-Hilbert theory using the path integral and BV formalisms. In this approach, the metric g μ ​ ν g^{\mu\nu} and the connection Γ μ ​ ν λ \Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu} are treated as independent, with the connection playing the role of an auxiliary field. We show that the gauge algebra is closed and irreducible. A novel trivial local symmetry arises, leading to structural identities that relate the Green’s functions of the auxiliary field to its classical value. We revisit the quantum equivalence between the first- and second-order formulations of the Einstein-Hilbert theory. By employing a suitable trick, a manifestly covariant form of the Senjanović measure is derived. We also show that the two formulations are equivalent at the level of the effective action when the auxiliary field is on-shell.



I
Introduction

General relativity can be formulated as a field theory of the metric
g^{\mu\nu}
. In the standard formulation, the Einstein-Hilbert (EH) action is given by

S^{(2)}[g]=-\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}}\int d^{4}x\,\sqrt{-g}\,g^{\mu\nu}R_{\mu\nu}(\Gamma),

(1)

where
\kappa^{2}=16\pi G_{N}
with
G_{N}
the Newton constant,
g=\det g_{\mu\nu}
, and the Ricci tensor is

R_{\mu\nu}(\Gamma)=\partial_{\nu}\Gamma_{\mu\lambda}{}^{\lambda}{}-\partial_{\lambda}\Gamma_{\mu\nu}{}^{\lambda}-\Gamma_{\mu\nu}{}^{\lambda}\Gamma_{\lambda\sigma}{}^{\sigma}+\Gamma_{\mu\lambda}{}^{\sigma}\Gamma_{\nu\sigma}{}^{\lambda},

(2)

with the Levi-Civita connection

\Gamma_{\mu\nu}{}^{\lambda}=\frac{1}{2}g^{\lambda\rho}\left(\partial_{\nu}g_{\mu\rho}+\partial_{\nu}g_{\nu\rho}-\partial_{\rho}g_{\mu\nu}\right).

(3)

From Eq. (
1
), one can derive the Einstein field equations, and the theory can be quantized using standard methods for gauge theories.

Note that the Lagrangian (
1
) contains second derivatives of the metric (arising from the Ricci scalar
R=R_{\mu\nu}g^{\mu\nu}
); we therefore refer to this as the
second-order
formulation of the Einstein–Hilbert theory.
However, if the metric
g^{\mu\nu}
and the connection
\Gamma_{\mu\nu}{}^{\lambda}
are treated as independent fields, the same action depends only on first-order derivatives. This defines the
first-order
(Palatini) formulation of the EH theory
34
. In this form, the Feynman rules simplify considerably, yielding only a cubic, momentum-independent interaction vertex. This makes the computation of quantum corrections more manageable
57
;
46
;
23
;
51
;
20
;
21
, and allows for computations of correlation functions of composite fields
14
;
15
;
1
.

The second-order EH theory can be quantized using the BRST formalism
12
;
66
or the path integral with the Faddeev-Popov procedure
32
, both of which are manifestly covariant. A more general method is the Batalin-Vilkovisky (BV) formalism
10
;
11
, which constructs the quantum action from the gauge algebra and can handle both closed and open algebras, as well as reducible ones
8
. In the second-order EH theory, the gauge algebra corresponds to diffeomorphisms, which is closed and irreducible
24
. In this case, the BV method reduces to the Faddeev-Popov quantization.

The covariant quantization of the EH theory in the second-order formulation within the BV formalism has been extensively studied in the literature (see, for example, the review
38
and more recent works
6
;
7
;
22
;
67
;
27
).
The BV formalism has also been extended to a variety of more general gravitational theories (see, e.g., Refs.
59
;
45
), including higher-derivative models
1
1

1

Higher-derivative gravitational models are known to suffer from ghosts. Interestingly, it has been proposed that such ghost may form bound states, thereby rendering the theory effectively stable
4
.
such as
R^{2}
gravity
63
and general
f(R)
theories
25
;
55
;
41
;
56
, as well as supergravity
43
;
54
.

Moreover, in the tetrad (first-order) formulation of gravity
3
;
5
;
40
;
42
, covariant quantization of Palatini gravity has also been received considerable attention
48
;
58
;
26
;
17
, including recent studies of its renormalization within the BV formalism and the background field method
18
.
In contrast, in the purely metric formulation, to the best of our knowledge, a complete covariant BV treatment has not yet been developed for the EH theory in first-order form.

Motivated by this, in the present work we study the BV quantization of the EH theory in first-order form using the metric as the fundamental field. We adopt the Goldberg parametrization
37
,

h^{\mu\nu}=\sqrt{\det g_{\mu\nu}}g^{\mu\nu},

(4)

and first analyze the gauge algebra of the theory, constructing the corresponding BV quantum action. Quantization is then performed within the path-integral formalism.
The first-order formulation introduces the connection as an auxiliary field, which generates constraints in the Dirac-Bergmann classification
30
;
13
. These are second-class constraints
44
;
52
. While first-class constraints correspond to gauge invariances, second-class constraints impose algebraic restrictions on phase space. Their presence requires the Senjanović procedure
61
rather than the usual Faddeev-Popov approach. Although this has been considered in Ref.
29
, it leads to a non-manifestly covariant path integral because the canonical structure of the classical theory is itself noncovariant.

One way to address this issue is the Batalin-Tyutin formalism
9
, which converts second-class constraints into first-class ones. Similarly, Faddeev and Shatashvili proposed introducing auxiliary fields, analogous to the Stueckelberg mechanism
64
for the Proca action, to obtain a covariant measure.
Another method to obtain a manifestly covariant expression for the Senjanović measure was previously proposed in Ref.
35
. Here, we provide a complete and covariant formulation of this result and extend it by incorporating a generating functional (with sources) for both the graviton and the auxiliary field.

Moreover, following Ref.
49
, we show that the first-order EH theory possesses a
trivial local symmetry
associated with the auxiliary field. This symmetry leads to structural identities relating the Green’s functions of the auxiliary field to its classical value obtained from the equations of motion. Similar identities have been derived and verified at one-loop order in Refs.
14
;
15
as a consequence of the equivalence between the generating functionals of the second- and first-order formulations. The identities derived here, however, involve only Green’s functions computed in the first-order formulation.
These identities arise intrinsically from the first-order formulation and provide the quantum realization of the classical equations of motion for the auxiliary field.

Finally, we revisit the quantum equivalence between the first- and second-order formulations of the EH theory. For reviews and detailed discussions, we refer the reader, for instance, to Refs.
2
;
15
;
35
;
19
. We show that the Senjanović measure derived here plays an important role in establishing the exact equivalence. We also extend this result to the quantum effective action
\Gamma

(recently demonstrated for the background effective action in Ref.
19
).

The paper is organized as follows. In Sec. II, we analyze the gauge algebra of both first- and second-order formulations, showing that both are closed and irreducible, and that a novel trivial symmetry emerges in the first-order formulation. In Sec. III, we construct the BV quantum action based on these results. In Sec. IV, we quantize the first-order theory starting from the generating functional of the second-order theory and then directly in first-order form, discussing their quantum equivalence. In Sec. V, we analyze the symmetries of the EH theory, focusing on the trivial symmetry in the first-order formulation, and derive structural identities from the invariance of the generating functional. Finally, in Sec. VI, we present a brief discussion of our results. In the Appendix A, we consider the contributions from the Senjanović measure in finite temperature. The Zinn-Justin master equations
69
for the EH theory in both first- and second-order are derived in Appendix B. Throughout this paper, right and left functional derivatives with respect to a field (or antifield) are denoted by “
\leftarrow
” and “
\rightarrow
”, respectively.

II
Gauge algebra

In this section, we analyze the gauge algebra of both the first and second order formulations of the EH theory. We also show that these theories are equivalent at the classical level. It is important to note that the quantization in the BV formalism depends on the structure of the gauge algebra. When the algebra is closed and irreducible, the BV formalism reduces to the Faddeev-Popov quantization.

II.1
Second-order formulation

We use the Goldberg metric
h^{\mu\nu}\equiv\sqrt{-g}g^{\mu\nu}
. We will also consider a weak field expansion
h^{\mu\nu}=\eta^{\mu\nu}+\kappa\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}
. The standard formulation of the EH theory using this parametrization can be written as [see Eq. (3.13) of Ref.
21
]

(5)

where, in
{D}
spacetime dimensions,

\begin{split}\mathchoice{(M^{-1})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{(M^{-1})^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textst (12)

and

\mathchoice{f^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}{f^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}{f^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}{f^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}}(x,y,z)=\delta(x-y)\delta_{\beta}^{\lambda}\partial_{\alpha}\delta(x-z)-\delta(x-z)\delta_{\alpha}^{\lambda}\partial_{\beta}\delta(x-y).

(13)

It is well-known that this algebra is closed and irreducible
24
.

II.2
First-order formulation

The first order formulation of the EH gravity reads
52

S^{(1)}[h,G]=\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}}\int\mathop{dx}\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{M^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makeb (15b)

(16)

Substituting Eq. (
16
) in the first-order Lagrangian (
14
), we obtain the EH Lagrangian in second order form (
5
). Note that, using the second equation of Eq. (
16
) into the first equation leads to the equations of motion of the second-order formulation shown in Eq. (
7
). This shows the classical equivalence between the first and second-order formulations of the EH theory.

S^{(1)}}{\delta G_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}{}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(z)}\frac{\delta S^{(1)}}{\delta G_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}{}^{\beta^{\prime}}(x)}\xi^{\lambda}(x)=0,\end{split}

(22)

where we have used that
\mathchoice{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=-\mathchoice{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta\alpha}}}_{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\disp .
This shows that the novel local symmetry (
20
) is not a genuine gauge symmetry, i.e., it is not associated with any degeneracy of the EH action.

The gauge algebra of the first order formulation of EH follows

\displaystyle[\delta_{\xi_{1}},\delta_{\xi_{2}}]h^{\mu\nu}(x)={}

\displaystyle\int\mathop{dw}\mathchoice{\mathcal{R}^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}(x,w;h)\zeta_{3}^{\lambda}(w),

(23)

\displaystyle[\delta_{\xi_{1}},\delta_{\xi_{2}}]\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(x)={}

\displaystyle\int\mathop{dw}\mathchoice{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle (24)

We remind that
\zeta_{3}
is the same parameter that appears in the second order gauge algebra and it is defined in Eq. (
12
). Similarly, as in the second order case, the first order gauge algebra (
23
) is closed and irreducible.

III
BV formalism

In order to derive the quantum action for EH theory in the first order form using the BV-formalism
10
;
11
, we introduce the set of fields
{\bm{\phi}}^{I}
and antifields
\prescript{\star}{}{\bm{\phi}}^{I}
(which forms an antisymplectic space)

{\bm{\phi}}^{I}=\begin{pmatrix}{}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}&\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}&d^{\mu}\end{pmatrix},\quad\prescript{\star}{}{\bm{\phi}}^{I}=\begin{pmatrix}\prescript{\star}{}{}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}&\prescript{\star}{}{G}\mathchoice{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}&\prescript{\star}{}{d}^{\mu}\end{pmatrix},

(25)

where
d^{\mu}
is the Faddeev-Popov ghost field. The antifields are the sources of the BRST transformations of the corresponding fields.

Now, we have to find a solution to the master equation

(S,S)\equiv\int\mathop{d^{4}x}S\frac{\overset{\leftarrow}{\delta}}{\delta{\bm{\phi}}^{I}}\frac{\overset{\rightarrow}{\delta}}{\delta\prescript{\star}{}{}{\bm{\phi}}^{I}}S=0

(26)

that satisfies the boundary condition

S\big|_{\prescript{\star}{}{\bm{\phi}}=0}=S^{(1)}[h,G].

(27)

We recall that the gauge algebra (
17
) is closed and irreducible. Thus, the quantum action is linear in the antifields, which reads

\begin{split}S={}&S^{(1)}[h,G]+\prescript{\star}{}{h}_{\mu\nu}[{}\mathfrak{h}^{\sigma\nu}\partial_{\sigma}d^{\mu}+{}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\lambda}\partial_{\lambda}d^{\nu}-\partial_{\lambda}({}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}d^{\lambda})]-\prescript{\star}{}{d}_{\mu}d^{\lambda}\partial_{\lambda}d^{\mu}\\
&+\kappa\prescript{\star}{}{G}\mathchoice{{}^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{{}^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{{}^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{{}^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}\bigg[-\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\delta^{\lambda}_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{\mu}^{\lambda}\partial_{\nu}+\delta_{\nu}^{\lambda}\partial_{\mu})\partial_{\rho}\delta^{\rho}_{\alpha}-\delta^{\rho}_{\alpha}\partial_{\rho}\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}+\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\partial_{\rho}\delta^{\lambda}_{\alpha}-(\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\partial_{\nu}+\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53847pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\nu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53847pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\nu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53847pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\nu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53847pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\nu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\partial_{\mu})\delta^{\rho}_{\alpha}\bigg]d^{\lambda}.\end{split}

(28)

Next, we have to introduce a gauge fixing term. For this, we extend the action by introducing the Nakanishi-Lautrup field
B^{\mu}

47
;
53
, the FP antighost
\bar{d}^{\mu}
and their corresponding antifields.
The resulting action
S^{\prime}=S[{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)},\prescript{\star}{}{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}]
in the extended antisymplectic space

{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)\,I}=\begin{pmatrix}{}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}&\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}&d^{\mu}&\bar{d}^{\mu}&B^{\mu}\end{pmatrix},\quad\prescript{\star}{}{}{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)\,I}=\begin{pmatrix}\prescript{\star}{}{}{}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}&\prescript{\star}{}{}\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}&\prescript{\star}{}{}d^{\mu}&\prescript{\star}{}{}\bar{d}^{\mu}&\prescript{\star}{}{}B^{\mu}\end{pmatrix}

(29)

is given by

\begin{split}S^{\prime}=S+\prescript{\star}{}{}\bar{d}^{\mu}B_{\mu}.\end{split}

(30)

The action (
30
) also satisfies the master equation

(S^{\prime},S^{\prime})=0,\quad S^{\prime}\big|_{\prescript{\star}{}{}{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}=0}=S^{(1)}[h,G].

(31)

We obtain the gauge fixed action
S_{\text{FP}}[{\bm{\phi}}]
by introducing a BRST exact fermionic function as follows:

S_{\text{FP}}^{(1)}[{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}]=S^{\prime}\left[{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)},\prescript{\star}{}{}{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}=\Psi\overset{\leftarrow}{\partial}_{{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}}\right],

(32)

where
\Psi=\Psi[{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}]
is the gauge-fixing potential.
Here, we will consider the De Donder gauge for the Goldberg metric. Thus, the corresponding gauge-fixing potential in a general gauge reads

\Psi={\bar{d}}_{\mu}\left(\partial_{\nu}{}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}+\frac{\xi}{2}B^{\mu}\right),

(33)

where
\xi
is a constant (analogous to
R_{\xi}
gauges in YM theories). When
\xi=0
, we have the Landau-DeWitt gauge, which is the singular case of Eq. (
33
). In the Landau-DeWitt gauge, the quadratic operator assumes minimal form and the effective action remains manifestly background gauge invariant, greatly simplifying one-loop analyses.

The gauge fixed action for the EH theory in first order form (
14
) is then given by

\begin{split}S_{\text{FP}}^{(1)}&[{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}]=S^{(1)}[h,G]+B^{\mu}\left(\partial_{\nu}{}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}+\frac{\xi}{2}B^{\mu}\right)\\
&+\bar{d}_{\mu}\left\{\partial^{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}+\kappa\left[(\partial_{\rho}\mathfrak{h}^{\rho\sigma})\partial_{\sigma}\eta^{\mu\nu}-(\partial_{\rho}\mathfrak{h}^{\rho\mu})\partial^{\nu}+\mathfrak{h}^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}\eta^{\mu\nu}-(\partial_{\rho}\partial^{\nu}\mathfrak{h}^{\rho\mu})\right]\right\}d_{\nu}.\end{split}

(34)

In the second-order formulation, the quantum action is similar:

\begin{split}S_{\text{FP}}^{(2)}&[{\bm{\phi}}^{(2)}]=S^{(2)}[h]+B^{\mu}\left(\partial_{\nu}{}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}+\frac{\xi}{2}B^{\mu}\right)\\
&+\bar{d}_{\mu}\left\{\partial^{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}+\kappa\left[(\partial_{\rho}\mathfrak{h}^{\rho\sigma})\partial_{\sigma}\eta^{\mu\nu}-(\partial_{\rho}\mathfrak{h}^{\rho\mu})\partial^{\nu}+\mathfrak{h}^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}\eta^{\mu\nu}-(\partial_{\rho}\partial^{\nu}\mathfrak{h}^{\rho\mu})\right]\right\}d_{\nu}.\end{split}

(35)

where

{\bm{\phi}}^{(2)\,I}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}&d^{\mu}&\bar{d}^{\mu}&B^{\mu}\end{pmatrix}
are fields of the extended antisymplectic
space in this form. Comparing the actions (
34
) and (
35
), we see that the Faddeev-Popov sector is equal in both cases. This is an interesting characteristic that can be explored to derive the quantum equivalence. However, the first-order form of gauge theories may accommodate more general gauge fixings due to the auxiliary field. Here, we will only consider the case of that
\Psi
depends on the graviton field
\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}
and
B^{\mu}
.

IV
Quantization

Now, we are ready to proceed with the quantization using the path integral formalism.
The generating functional of the first order formulation of the EH theory is given by

Z^{(2)}[\bm{j}]=\frac{1}{N}\int\mathcal{D}{\bm{\phi}}^{(2)}\exp\left[i\left(S_{\text{FP}}^{(2)}[{\bm{\phi}}^{(2)}]+\int\mathop{dx}\bm{j}\cdot\bm{\mathfrak{h}}\right)\right].

(36)

where
N
is a normalization factor,
\bm{j}=\{j_{\mu\nu}\}
is the external source for the field
\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}
in matrix form (similar notation is employed to the fields
\bm{\mathfrak{h}}=\{\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}\}
) and
\bm{A}\cdot\bm{B}\cdot\bm{C}\equiv\mathop{\rm Tr}(\bm{ABC})
.

To obtain the generating functional in the first-order form, we employ a trick proposed in
35
. The procedure starts by inserting the constant factor

1=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bm{G}}\Delta^{1/2}(h,J)\exp i\int\mathop{dx}\left(\frac{1}{2\kappa^{2}}\bm{G}\cdot\bm{M}\cdot\bm{G}+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}\bm{J}\cdot\bm{M}^{-1}\cdot\bm{J}\right)

(37)

into an extension of the generating functional (
36
):

Z^{(2)}[\bm{j};\bm{J}]=\frac{1}{N}\int\mathcal{D}{\bm{\phi}}^{(2)}\exp\left[i\left(S_{\text{FP}}^{(2)}[{\bm{\phi}}^{(2)}]+\int\mathop{dx}(\bm{j}\cdot\bm{\mathfrak{h}}+\bm{J}\cdot\bm{\mathcal{G}})\right)\right]

(38)

in which an additional source for the classical value of the auxiliary field
\mathcal{G}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}(h)
is introduced.

Then, we redefine the field
\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
by a shift:

\bm{G}\to\bm{G}-\bm{\mathcal{G}}+\kappa^{2}\bm{M}^{-1}(h)\cdot\bm{J}

(39)

leading to

\begin{split}\frac{1}{N}\int&\mathop{\mathcal{D}{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}}\Delta^{1/2}(h,J)\exp\left\{i\left[S_{\text{FP}}^{(1)}[{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}]+\int\mathop{dx}\left(\bm{j}\cdot\bm{\mathfrak{h}}+\bm{J}\cdot\bm{G}+\kappa^{2}\bm{J}\cdot\bm{M}^{-1}(h)\cdot\bm{J}\right)\right]\right\}.\end{split}

(40)

The new factor
\Delta^{1/2}(h,J)
corresponds to the Senjanović determinant
61
. To derive the explicit form of this factor, we integrate the auxiliary field in Eq. (
37
) arriving at

\Delta^{1/2}(h,J)=|\det\mathchoice{M^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}}{M^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 4.12852pt{\rho}\kern 4.2152pt}}}{M^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}}{M^{{{\mu\nu}{\pi\tau}}}_{{{\lambda}\kern 3.60768pt{\rho}\kern 3.93353pt}}}(h)|^{1/2}\exp i\int\mathop{dx}\left(-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}\bm{J}\cdot\bm{M}^{-1}\cdot\bm{J}\right),

(41)

where the first term is the Senjanović determinant. This determinant arises due to the presence of second-class constraints associated with the auxiliary field
\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}

52
. Using this trick, we obtain a manifestly covariant result for the Senjanović determinant (cf. with the measure in Eq. (64) of Ref.
29
).

Now, substituting Eq. (
41
) into Eq. (
40
), we find the generating functional in first order form:

Z^{(1)}[\bm{j},\bm{J}]=\frac{1}{N}\int\mathcal{D}{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}|\det\bm{M}(h)|^{1/2}\exp\left\{i\left[S_{\text{FP}}^{(1)}[{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}]+\int\mathop{dx}\left(\bm{j}\cdot\bm{\mathfrak{h}}+\bm{J}\cdot\bm{G}+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}\bm{J}\cdot\bm{M}^{-1}(h)\cdot\bm{J}\right)\right]\right\},

(42)

where
\bm{j}=\{j^{\mu\nu}\}
and
\bm{J}=\{\mathchoice{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}\}
denote external sources written in matrix notation. An analogous notation is used for the auxiliary field
\bm{G}=\{\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\}
. The resulting expression is manifestly covariant, in contrast with formulations obtained by applying the Senjanović-Faddeev procedure directly (see, for example, Ref.
29
;
2
).

Note that in the literature, the Senjanović determinant is usually ignored
28
due to its apparent triviality. Indeed,
\det\bm{M}(h)
gives rise to a set of massless tadpole-like contributions that vanish in dimensional regularization
16
. However, as we have demonstrated, it plays an important role in understanding the quantum equivalence of the two formulations. At finite temperature (see Appendix
A
), this remains true, since the determinant is required to cancel the contributions from the auxiliary field in the free energy
F
at functional level.

IV.1
Quantum equivalence

Therefore, we find the first order form of the
generating functional of the second-order formulation EH theory (
36
). The quantum equivalence follows as a consequence of the general relation

Z^{(1)}[\bm{j},\bm{J}]=Z^{(2)}[\bm{j};\bm{J}]

(43)

that implies that

Z^{(1)}[\bm{j},0]=Z^{(2)}[\bm{j}].

(44)

This means that the first- and second-order formulations of the EH theory share identical gauge field Green’s functions:

\langle 0|T\mathfrak{h}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}(x_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{h}^{\mu_{n}\nu_{n}}(x_{n})|0\rangle^{(1)}=\langle 0|T\mathfrak{h}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}(x_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{h}^{\mu_{n}\nu_{n}}(x_{n})|0\rangle^{(2)},

(45)

where the labels
(1)
and
(2)
mark the Green’s function computed in the first and second order formulations respectively.

Moreover, it has been shown that the use of a relation analogous to
Eq. (
43
) (see Refs.
14
;
15
), allows one to derive several structural identities that connect Green’s functions computed in the first-order formulation with their counterparts in the second-order formulation. For example, we have that

\displaystyle\langle These structural identities are complementary to the usual Ward identities satisfied by gauge theories.
