.
In the Sect. V, we show that these stronger identities (and many others) can be derived by using the invariance of the generating functional under the trivial local symmetry (
20
).

By employing the expression

\det\bm{M}^{1/2}=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bm{H}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bm{\theta}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\bm{{\theta}}}}\exp\int\mathop{dx}{\left(\frac{1}{2}\bm{H}\cdot\bm{M}\cdot\bm{H}+\bar{\bm{\theta}}\cdot\bm{M}\cdot\bm{\theta}\right)}

(48)

to exponentiate the Senjanović determinant in Eq. (
42
), we obtain the local form of the generating functional of the EH theory in first order:

Z^{(1)}[\bm{j},\bm{J}]=\int\mathcal{D}{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bm{H}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bm{\theta}}\mathop{\mathcal{D}\bar{\bm{{\theta}}}}\exp\left\{i\left[S_{\text{FP}}^{(1)}[{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}]+\int\mathop{dx}\left(\frac{1}{2}\bm{H}\cdot\bm{M}(h)\cdot\bm{H}+\bar{\bm{\theta}}\cdot\bm{M}(h)\cdot\bm{\theta}+\bm{j}\cdot\bm{\mathfrak{h}}+\bm{J}\cdot\bm{G}\right)\right]\right\},

(49)

where
\mathchoice{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
is a bosonic field and
\mathchoice{\theta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\theta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\theta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\theta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
,
\mathchoice{\bar{\theta}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{\theta}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{\theta}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{\theta}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
are fermionic fields.

It is worth noting that the explicit verification of the structural identities presented in Ref.
15
remains valid, since the contributions of the additional fields
\bm{H}
and
\bm{\theta}
,
\bm{\bar{\theta}}
vanish when dimensional regularization
50
is employed. In contrast, Ref.
35
demonstrates that, once these contributions are properly included, the structural identities hold already at the integrand level, independently of the choice of gauge or regularization scheme. This behavior is a direct manifestation of the quantum equivalence between the first- and second-order formulations, rather than a consequence of specific regularization-dependent cancellations.

Moreover, these fields allow computation of the number of degrees of freedom of the first-order EH theory directly from the path integral. Denoting by
N_{F}
the number of degrees of freedom associated with a field
F
, the total number of degrees of freedom in the first-order formulation follows straightforwardly:

N^{(1)}=N_{\bm{\phi}^{(1)}}+N_{{H}}-2N_{{\theta}}=N_{\bm{\phi}^{(2)}}+N_{{G}}+N_{{H}}-2N_{{\theta}}.

(50)

Note that,
N_{{G}}=N_{H}=N_{\theta}
(the minus comes from the ghost-like character of the field
\theta
). Thus,

N^{(1)}=N_{\bm{\phi}^{(2)}}\equiv N^{(2)}=N_{\mathfrak{h}}-2N_{d}=2,

(51)

which correspond to the two physical polarizations of the graviton. This shows that, upon inclusion of the Senjanović determinant, the first- and second-order EH formulations share the same number of physical degrees of freedom.

IV.1.1
Effective action

Defining the connected generating functional as

W[j]=-i\ln Z[j]
,
the classical field
\bar{\phi}
is given by

\bar{\phi}(x)=\frac{\delta W[j]}{\delta j(x)}.

(52)

The effective action
\Gamma[\bar{\phi}]
is obtained from
W[j]
by a Legendre transformation,

\Gamma[\bar{\phi}]=W[j]-\int\mathop{dx}j(x)\bar{\phi}(x).

(53)

In our case, using that
W^{(1)}[\bm{j},\bm{J}]=W^{(2)}[\bm{j};\bm{J}]
, we have that

\bar{\mathfrak{h}}^{\mu\nu}=\frac{\delta W^{(1)}[\bm{j},\bm{J}]}{\delta j_{\mu\nu}}=\frac{\delta W^{(2)}[\bm{j};\bm{J}]}{\delta j_{\mu\nu}}

(54)

and

\mathchoice{\bar{G}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{G}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{G}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{G}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}+\kappa^{2}(\bm{M}^{-1}\bm{J})\mathchoice{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=\frac{\delta W^{(1)}}{\delta\mathchoice{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}}=\frac{\delta W^{(2)}}{\delta\mathchoice{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}}=\mathchoice{\bar{\mathcal{G}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{\mathcal{G}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{\mathcal{G}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{\mathcal{G}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[h].

(55)

The effective action of the first-order EH theory is given by

\Gamma^{(1)}[\bar{\mathfrak{h}},\bar{G}]=W^{(1)}[\bm{j},\bm{J}]-\int\mathop{dx}\left(\bm{j}\cdot\bar{\bm{\mathfrak{h}}}+\bm{J}\cdot\bar{\bm{G}}+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}\bm{J}\cdot\bm{M}^{-1}\cdot\bm{J}\right).

(56)

The effective action corresponding to the generating functional
Z^{(2)}[\bm{j};\bm{J}]
reads

\Gamma^{(2)}[\bar{\mathfrak{h}};\bar{G}]=W^{(2)}[\bm{j};\bm{J}]-\int\mathop{dx}(\bm{j}\cdot\bar{\bm{\mathfrak{h}}}+\bm{J}\cdot\bar{\bm{\mathcal{G}}}).

(57)

From Eq. (
55
), we find that

\Gamma^{(1)}[\bar{\mathfrak{h}},\bar{G}]=\Gamma^{(2)}[\bar{\mathfrak{h}};\bar{\mathcal{G}}]+\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\bm{G}}-\bar{\bm{\mathcal{G}}})\cdot\bm{M}\cdot(\bar{\bm{G}}-\bar{\bm{\mathcal{G}}}).

(58)

This relates the one-particle irreducible Green’s function of the first- and second-order formulations of the EH theory.
Moreover, when
\bm{J}=0
, the effective actions coincide:

\Gamma^{(1)}[\bar{\mathfrak{h}},\bar{G}=\bar{\mathcal{G}(\bar{\mathfrak{h}}}]=\Gamma^{(2)}[\bar{\mathfrak{h}};\bar{\mathcal{G}}]\equiv\Gamma^{(2)}[\bar{\mathfrak{h}}],

(59)

where
\Gamma^{(2)}[\bar{\mathfrak{h}}]
is the effective action of the second-order formulation of the EH theory.
Imposing
\bm{J}=0
in Eq. (
55
) implies that the auxiliary field
\bar{\bm{G}}
is on-shell. This result was demonstrated for the background effective action in Ref.
19
.

V
Symmetries and structural identities

The Faddeev-Popov action is endowed with the well-known BRST symmetry. When we fix a gauge, the initial gauge symmetry is broken.
However, the gauge symmetry is not entirely lost but survives globally as the BRST symmetry, which reflects the gauge structure of the classical action at the quantum level.

The BRST symmetry of the action (
34
) reads

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}=h^{\mu\rho}\partial_{\rho}d^{\nu}-h^{\nu\rho}\partial_{\rho}d^{\mu}-\partial_{\rho}(h^{\mu\nu}d^{\rho}),

(60a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}{G}{{}_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}}=\kappa\bigg[-\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}d^{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{\mu}^{\lambda}\partial_{\nu}+\delta_{\nu}^{\lambda}\partial_{\mu})\partial_{\rho}d^{\rho}-d^{\rho}\partial_{\rho}\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}+\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\partial_{\rho}d^{\lambda}-(\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\partial_{\nu}+\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53847pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\nu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53847pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\nu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53847pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\nu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53847pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\nu\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\partial_{\mu})d^{\rho}\bigg],

(60b)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}d^{\mu}=\kappa d^{\rho}\partial_{\rho}d^{\mu},

(60c)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\bar{d}^{\mu}=B^{\mu}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{s}B^{\mu}=0,

(60d)

where
\mathsf{s}
is the nilpotent BRST operator,
\mathsf{s}^{2}=0
.
The invariance of the generating functional (
42
),
\mathsf{s}Z^{(1)}=0
, leads to the corresponding Ward identities.

Similarly, the same considerations apply to the second-order form, where the BRST symmetry takes the following form
24

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}=h^{\mu\rho}\partial_{\rho}d^{\nu}-h^{\nu\rho}\partial_{\rho}d^{\mu}-\partial_{\rho}(h^{\mu\nu}d^{\rho}),

(61a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}d^{\mu}=\kappa d^{\rho}\partial_{\rho}d^{\mu},

(61b)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\bar{d}^{\mu}=B^{\mu}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{s}B^{\mu}=0.

(61c)

One can obtain the Slavnov-Taylor identities
62
;
65
using the invariance of the generating functional under the BRST symmetry for both second- and first-order formulations of the EH theory. For completeness, we have included their master equations in the Appendix B.

We now consider the additional symmetry (
20
) found in the first-order formulation of the EH theory. This symmetry is not relevant for quantization, as it does not arise from the degeneracy of the classical action. However, at the quantum level, it leads to interesting identities between the gauge and auxiliary fields.
Similar identities were derived in Refs.
14
;
15
using the relation (
43
).
In contrast, we will see that the identities derived here follow directly from the first-order formulation itself, without reference to the second-order form of the EH theory. This provides an intrinsic derivation based solely on the underlying trivial local symmetry found in the EH theory in first-order form.

We observe that the generating functional (
42
) must be invariant under the local symmetry (
20
):

\delta_{\xi}Z^{(1)}[\bm{j},\bm{J}]=0.

(62)

Note that the measure of
Z^{(1)}
remains invariant under this transformation. Therefore,

\int\mathcal{D}{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}|\det\bm{M}(h)|^{1/2}{\left(\bm{J}\cdot\delta_{\xi}\bm{G}\right)}\exp\left[i\left(S_{\text{FP}}^{(1)}[{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}]+\int\mathop{dx}\left(\bm{j}\cdot\bm{\mathfrak{h}}+\bm{J}\cdot\bm{G}+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}\bm{J}\cdot\bm{M}^{-1}(h)\cdot\bm{J}\right)\right)\right]=0

(63)

This can be rewritten in the form

\langle 0|T\delta_{\xi}\bm{G}|0\rangle_{\bm{j},\bm{J}}^{(1)}=0,

(64)

where we have

\langle 0|T\mathcal{O}(\ldots)|0\rangle^{(1)}_{\bm{j},\bm{J}}\equiv\int\mathcal{D}{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}|\det\bm{M}(h)|^{1/2}\mathcal{O}(\ldots)\exp\left[i\left(S_{\text{FP}}^{(1)}[{\bm{\phi}}^{(1)}]+\int\mathop{dx}\left(\bm{j}\cdot\bm{\mathfrak{h}}+\bm{J}\cdot\bm{G}+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}\bm{J}\cdot\bm{M}^{-1}(h)\cdot\bm{J}\right)\right)\right].

(65)

In order to obtain a master equation for the identities that arises from Eq. (
64
), we have to introduce a source
\mathchoice{K^{{{\pi\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}{K^{{{\pi\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}{K^{{{\pi\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}{K^{{{\pi\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}}
for the classical value
\mathchoice{\mathcal{G}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}_{{{\pi\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{G}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}_{{{\pi\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{G}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}_{{{\pi\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{G}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.36418pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.93517pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52512pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}}}_{{{\pi\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
in the generating functional (
49
), such that,

\frac{1}{i}\frac{\delta}{\delta\bm{K}(x)}Z^{(1)}[\bm{j},\bm{J};\bm{K}]=\langle 0|T\bm{\mathcal{G}}(x)|0\rangle_{\bm{j},\bm{J};\bm{K}}^{(1)}.

(66)

Now, Eq. (
64
) reads

\int\mathop{dx}\mathop{dy}\mathchoice{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha\beta}}}_{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\left(\frac{\delta Z^{(1)}[\bm{j},\bm{J};\bm{K}]}{\delta\mathchoice{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(y)}\right)\zeta^{\lambda}(x)=0.

(67)

Since
\zeta
is an arbitrary parameter, we get

\left[\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathchoice{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}(x)}-\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathchoice{K^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{K^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.7 0\left|T\right|0\right\rangle_{\bm{j},\bm{J};\bm{K}}^{(1)}=0,

(68)

which constitutes the master equation for the
intrinsic structural identities
associated with the trivial symmetry (
20
).
We refer to these relations as intrinsic structural identities, since they arise directly from the first-order formulation without invoking quantum equivalence.

The functional differentiation of Eq. (
68
) with respect to

) with respect to
\mathchoice{j^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{j^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{j^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}{j^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha\beta}}}}(y)
, followed by setting all sources to zero.
Further identities can be obtained by successive functional differentiation of the master equation (
68
), thereby generating a hierarchy of interdependent constraints.
This hierarchy is analogous to the Dyson-Schwinger equations
31
;
60
, which restrict the Green’s functions by imposing the invariance of the generating functional
Z
under general fields redefinitions.

(72)

which underlies the trivial local symmetry.
Consequently, the intrinsic structural identities derived from it can be interpreted as the quantum realization of above equation.
As a simple example, setting the sources to zero in Eq. (
71
) yields

\langle 0|T\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(x)|0\rangle^{(1)}=\langle 0|T\mathchoice{\mathcal{G}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{G}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{G}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{G}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[h](x)|0\rangle^{(1)},

(73)

which directly reflects Eq. (
72
) at the quantum level.

To establish a connection between the intrinsic structural identities obtained here and those derived in Ref.
15
through the equivalence of generating functionals, we consider the extension of the generating functional of the EH theory in its second-order form, given in (
38
).
As a consequence of the quantum equivalence relation (
43
), Eq. (
68
) can be recast as the following relation:

\frac{\delta}{\delta\mathchoice{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}{J^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}}(x)}\left[\left\langle 0\left|T\right|0\right\rangle_{\bm{j},\bm{J}}^{(1)}-\left\langle 0\left|T\right|0\right\rangle_{\bm{j};\bm{J}}^{(2)}\right]=0,

(74)

which leads to the structural identities (
46a
) and (
46b
), and their higher-order generalizations.

VI
Conclusions

In this paper, we have studied the covariant quantization of the first-order formulation of EH gravity.
We analyzed the gauge algebra of both first- and second-order formulations, showing that they are closed and irreducible. In the first-order case, we identified a novel trivial symmetry associated with the auxiliary connection. Although irrelevant for classical gauge fixing, this symmetry leads to nontrivial quantum identities.

Using the BV formalism, we constructed the quantum action and derived a manifestly covariant expression for the Senjanović determinant, thereby avoiding the non-covariant measures of earlier approaches.
This, in turn, allows us to establish the exact quantum equivalence between the first- and second-order formulations at the level of the generating functional
Z
and the effective action
\Gamma
.

We then examine the trivial local symmetry (
20
) and show that it generates a family of structural identities [see, for example, Eqs. (
47
)] that constrain the Green’s functions of the auxiliary field. These identities arise directly from the first-order formulation and do not rely on the quantum equivalence relation (
43
). Although they can be related to the identities derived in Ref.
15
through it(
43
), they arising intrinsically within the first-order formulation of the EH theory.

At the same time, these identities are not independent among themselves, but instead form a hierarchy generated by the master equation (
68
). This structure is analogous to the well-known Slavnov–Taylor identities and is complementary to them (see Appendix B for their master equations).
Moreover, these identities can be interpreted as the quantum realization of the classical equations of motion (
72
).

Looking ahead, the ideas developed here may be extended to supergravity, where first-order formulations arise naturally
68
, and to theories with more complicated auxiliary field structures
33
;
36
. The structural identities may provide insight into nonperturbative aspects of quantum gravity, since they are exact relations between field correlations. Moreover, the role of the Senjanović determinant in curved spacetime and at finite temperature also deserves further investigation.

We thank J. Frenkel, F. T. Brand, D. G. C. McKeon for enlightening conversations.

Appendix A
Senjanović determinant in finite temperature

Consider the one-loop contributions to the free energy at finite temperature shown in Fig.
1
.

Figure 1:
Diagrams (a) are the one-loop contribution to the free energy in the second-order formulation of the EH theory. Diagrams (b) are the contributions that arise in the first-order EH theory. Wavy lines represent the graviton field
\mathbf{\mathfrak{h}}
, dotted lines are the Faddeev-Popov ghost fields
\bm{\bar{d}}
,
\bm{d}
and solid lines denote the auxiliary field
\mathbf{G}
. The doubled solid, dashed lines denote respectively the fields
\mathbf{H}
,
\bar{\bm{\theta}}
and
\bm{\theta}
, which arise from the Senjanović determinant
\det^{1/2}\bm{M}(h)
.

In the second-order formulation of the EH theory, we have:

F_{\text{one-loop}}^{(2)}\equiv-\ln Z^{(2)}\big|_{\text{one-loop}}=\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\rm Tr}\ln\frac{\delta^{2}S^{(2)}_{\text{FP}}}{\delta\bm{\mathfrak{h}}\delta\bm{\mathfrak{h}}}\bigg|_{\phi^{(2)}=0}-\mathop{\rm Tr}\ln\frac{\delta^{2}S^{(2)}_{\text{FP}}}{\delta\bm{\bar{d}}\delta\bm{d}}\bigg|_{\phi^{(2)}=0}.

(75)

In the first-order formulation, it reads:

F_{\text{one-loop}}^{(1)}=\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\rm Tr}\ln\frac{\delta^{2}S^{(1)}_{\text{FP}}}{\delta\bm{\hat{F}}\delta\bm{\hat{F}}}\bigg|_{\phi^{(1)}=0}-\mathop{\rm Tr}\ln\frac{\delta^{2}S^{(1)}_{\text{FP}}}{\delta\bm{\bar{d}}\delta\bm{d}}\bigg|_{\phi^{(1)}=0}-\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\rm Tr}\ln\bm{M}(h),

(76)

where the last term arises from the Senjanović determinant and we have defined

\bm{\hat{F}}=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{\mathfrak{h}}&\bm{G}\end{pmatrix}
.

Diagonalizing the first term on the right-hand side of Eq. (
76
) gives

\mathop{\rm Tr}\ln\frac{\delta^{2}S^{(1)}_{\text{FP}}}{\delta\bm{\hat{F}}\delta\bm{\hat{F}}}\bigg|_{\phi^{(1)}=0}=\mathop{\rm Tr}\ln\frac{\delta^{2}S^{(2)}_{\text{FP}}}{\delta\bm{\mathfrak{h}}\delta\bm{\mathfrak{h}}}\bigg|_{\phi^{(2)}=0}+\mathop{\rm Tr}\ln\frac{\delta^{2}S^{(1)}_{\text{FP}}}{\delta\bm{G}\delta\bm{G}}\bigg|_{\phi^{(1)}=0}.

(77)

Using this in Eq. (
76
), we then obtain that

F^{(2)}_{\text{one-loop}}=F^{(1)}_{\text{one-loop}},

(78)

since the ghost sector is the same in both formulations. We see that the Senjanović determinant cancels the auxiliary field
\bm{G}
contributions to the free energy at finite temperature. This illustrates the essential role of the Senjanović determinant in ensuring the quantum equivalence between the two formulations.

Appendix B
Master equations for the BRST symmetry

In this Appendix, we show the master equation for the BRST symmetries of the second- (
61
) and first-order Eq. (
60
) formulations of the EH theory.

B.1
Second-order formulation

We obtain the Slavnov-Taylor identities by extending the source term. In this formulation, the extended source term is given by

j_{\mu\nu}\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}+i(\bar{\eta}_{\mu}d^{\mu}-\bar{d}^{\mu}\eta_{\mu})+j_{\mu}B^{\mu}+K_{\mu\nu}\left[h^{\mu\rho}\partial_{\rho}d^{\nu}+h^{\nu\rho}\partial_{\rho}d^{\mu}-\partial_{\rho}(h^{\mu\nu}d^{\rho})\right]+\kappa K_{\mu}d^{\rho}\partial_{\rho}d^{\mu}.

(79)

Note that, we have added sources to the non-linear BRST variations, these sources in the BV formalism are the antifields.

The corresponding Zinn-Justin master equation is given by
35

{\left(\Gamma^{(2)},\Gamma^{(2)}\right)}=\int\mathop{dx}\left(\Gamma^{(2)}\frac{\overset{\leftarrow}{\delta}}{\delta\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}}\frac{\overset{\rightarrow}{\delta}\Gamma^{(2)}}{\delta K_{\mu\nu}}+\Gamma^{(2)}\frac{\overset{\leftarrow}{\delta}}{\delta d^{\mu}}\frac{\overset{\rightarrow}{\delta}\Gamma^{(2)}}{\delta K_{\mu}}+\Gamma^{(2)}\frac{\overset{\leftarrow}{\delta}}{\delta\bar{d}^{\mu}}\frac{\overset{\rightarrow}{\delta}\Gamma^{(2)}}{\delta j_{\mu}}\right)=0.

(80)

The Slavnov-Taylor identities of the second-order formulation of gravity are derived by taking functional derivatives of the master equation Eq. (
80
).

B.2
First-order formulation

In the first-order form, the extended source term reads

\begin{split}&j^{\mu\nu}\mathfrak{h}_{\mu\nu}+{J}{{}_{\lambda}^{\mu\nu}}\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\lambda}}}_{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}+i(\bar{\eta}_{\mu}d^{\mu}-\bar{d}^{\mu}\eta_{\mu})+j_{\mu}B^{\mu}\\

The corresponding Zinn-Justin master equation is given by

{\left(\Gamma^{(1)},\Gamma^{(1)}\right)}=\int\mathop{dx}\left(\Gamma^{(1)}\frac{\overset{\leftarrow}{\delta}}{\delta\mathfrak{h}^{\mu\nu}}\frac{\overset{\rightarrow}{\delta}\Gamma^{(1)}}{\delta d^{\mu}}\frac{\overset{\rightarrow}{\delta}\Gamma^{(1)}}{\delta K_{\mu}}+\Gamma^{(1)}\frac{\overset{\leftarrow}{\delta}}{\delta\bar{d}^{\mu}}\frac{\overset{\rightarrow}{\delta}\Gamma^{(1)}}{\delta (82)

The Slavnov-Taylor identities of the first-order formulation of gravity are derived by taking functional derivatives of the master equation Eq. (
82
).
